OUTPUT: set turn="review" and return exactly one item in the "review" array; leave every other array empty.
REVIEW TURN. Independently audit EVERY candidate in `candidates`; each is a
hypothesis from the discovery turn, not a trusted conclusion. Return exactly one
decision per candidate_id.

Return obligation_class (execution_compatibility per D4, target_coverage per D5),
scope_basis, trigger_basis and the five checks of J1, each with status
established/refuted/unknown, a specific reason and exact evidence:
scope_contract (J1a, D2); feasible_trigger (J1b): for execution compatibility a
finite actual target execution, for target coverage a valid supported
behavior/setup whose route the target blocks; observable_consequence (J1c);
alternatives_ruled_out (J1d, D8), including counterevidence supplied with the
candidate; identity_preserved (J1e; for timing also T1).

Use effect blocked_target_coverage only for target_coverage. For target_coverage
fill every coverage_witness field; for the other class use empty strings. These
fields do not replace the citations of each check.

decision=keep requires all five checks established with source and harness
evidence, a finite_witness and no decisive missing fact. decision=dismiss requires
a grounded refutation or equivalent route (J6). Otherwise return uncertain and
request the missing facts (E3). For maintenance, unresolved timing does not
invalidate a currently established obligation (J2).

Then audit the whole scope again (J4): assess every selected change ID and the
four foundations with evidence, challenge both dismissals (J6) and retained
gaps (J1). Out-of-scope items (D7) are not obligations. New evidence contains
contracts retrieved for earlier requests (`evidence`); `citation_errors` lists earlier
citations the program could not verify; cite valid locations instead. Request
exact symbols or paths only for decisive missing facts (E3).
If the evidence establishes a distinct obligation that is NOT among `candidates`
(for example one that only became visible with the new evidence), report it in
`new_candidates` with the same fields as a discovery blocker and mark its change
group as blocked in the scope audit. It is reviewed in a separate turn; do not
add a decision for it here.

It features an extremely fast decoder, with speed in\n51|  multiple GB/s per core, typically reaching RAM speed limits on multi-core systems.\n52|\n53|  The LZ4 compression library provides in-memory compression and decompression functions.\n54|  It gives full buffer control to user.\n55|  Compression can be done in:\n56|    - a single step (described as Simple Functions)\n57|    - a single step, reusing a context (described in Advanced Functions)\n58|    - unbounded multiple steps (described as Streaming compression)\n59|\n60|  lz4.h generates and decodes LZ4-compressed blocks (doc/lz4_Block_format.md).\n61|  Decompressing such a compressed block requires additional metadata.\n62|  Exact metadata depends on exact decompression function.\n63|  For the typical case of LZ4_decompress_safe(),\n64|  metadata includes block's compressed size, and maximum bound of decompressed size.\n65|  Each application is free to encode and pass such metadata in whichever way it wants.\n66|\n67|  lz4.h only handle blocks, it can not generate Frames.\n68|\n69|  Blocks are different from Frames (doc/lz4_Frame_format.md).\n70|  Frames bundle both blocks and metadata in a specified manner.\n71|  Embedding metadata is required for compressed data to be self-contained and portable.\n72|  Frame format is delivered through a companion API, declared in lz4frame.h.\n73|  The `lz4` CLI can only manage frames.\n74|*/\n75|\n76|/*^***************************************************************\n77|*  Export parameters\n78|*****************************************************************/\n79|/*\n80|*  LZ4_DLL_EXPORT :\n81|*  Enable exporting of functions when building a Windows DLL\n82|*  LZ4LIB_VISIBILITY :\n83|*  Control library symbols visibility.\n84|*/\n85|#ifndef LZ4LIB_VISIBILITY\n86|#  if defined(__GNUC__) && (__GNUC__ >= 4)\n87|#    define LZ4LIB_VISIBILITY __attribute__ ((visibility (\"default\")))\n88|#  else\n89|#    define LZ4LIB_VISIBILITY\n90|#  endif\n91|#endif\n92|#if defined(LZ4_DLL_EXPORT) && (LZ4_DLL_EXPORT==1)\n93|#  define LZ4LIB_API __declspec(dllexport) saving a function pointer load from the IAT and an indirect jump.*/\n96|#else\n97|#  define LZ4LIB_API LZ4LIB_VISIBILITY\n98|#endif\n99|\n100|/*! LZ4_FREESTANDING :\n101| *  When this macro is set to 1, it enables \"freestanding mode\" that is\n102| *  suitable for typical freestanding environment which doesn't support\n103| *  standard C library.\n104| *\n105| *  - LZ4_FREESTANDING is a compile-time switch.\n106| *  - It requires the following macros to be defined:\n107| *    LZ4_memcpy, LZ4_memmove, LZ4_memset.\n108| *  - It only enables LZ4/HC functions which don't use heap.\n109| *    All LZ4F_* functions are not supported.\n110| *  - See tests/freestanding.c to check its basic setup.\n111| */\n112|#if defined(LZ4_FREESTANDING) && (LZ4_FREESTANDING == 1)\n113|#  define LZ4_HEAPMODE 0\n114|#  define LZ4HC_HEAPMODE 0\n115|#  define LZ4_STATIC_LINKING_ONLY_DISABLE_MEMORY_ALLOCATION 1\n116|#  if !defined(LZ4_memcpy)\n117|#    error \"LZ4_FREESTANDING requires macro 'LZ4_memcpy'.\"\n118|#  endif\n119|#  if !defined(LZ4_memset)\n120|#    error \"LZ4_FREESTANDING requires macro 'LZ4_memset'.\"\n121|#  endif\n122|#  if !defined(LZ4_memmove)\n123|#    error \"LZ4_FREESTANDING requires macro 'LZ4_memmove'.\"\n124|#  endif\n125|#elif ! defined(LZ4_FREESTANDING)\n126|#  define LZ4_FREESTANDING 0\n127|#endif\n128|\n129|\n130|/*------   Version   ------*/\n131|#define LZ4_VERSION_MAJOR    1    /* for breaking interface changes  */\n132|#define LZ4_VERSION_MINOR   10    /* for new (non-breaking) interface capabilities */\n133|#define LZ4_VERSION_RELEASE  0    /* for tweaks, bug-fixes, or development */\n134|\n135|#define LZ4_VERSION_NUMBER (LZ4_VERSION_MAJOR *100*100 + LZ4_VERSION_MINOR *100 + LZ4_VERSION_RELEASE)\n136|\n137|#define LZ4_LIB_VERSION LZ4_VERSION_MAJOR.LZ4_VERSION_MINOR.LZ4_VERSION_RELEASE\n138|#define LZ4_QUOTE(str) #str\n139|#define LZ4_EXPAND_AND_QUOTE(str) LZ4_QUOTE(str)\n140|#define LZ4_VERSION_STRING LZ4_EXPAND_AND_QUOTE(LZ4_LIB_VERSION)  /* requires v1.7.3+ */\n141|\n142|LZ4LIB_API int LZ4_versionNumber (void);  /**< library version number; useful to check dll version; requires v1.3.0+ */\n143|LZ4LIB_API const char* LZ4_versionString (void);   /**< library version string; useful to check dll version; requires v1.7.5+ */\n144|\n145|\n146|/*-************************************\n147|*  Tuning memory usage\n148|**************************************/\n149|/*!\n150| * LZ4_MEMORY_USAGE :\n151| * Can be selected at compile time, by setting LZ4_MEMORY_USAGE.\n152| * Memory usage formula : N->2^N Bytes (examples : 10 -> 1KB; 12 -> 4KB ; 16 -> 64KB; 20 -> 1MB)\n153| * Increasing memory usage improves compression ratio, generally at the cost of speed.\n154| * Reduced memory usage may improve speed at the cost of ratio, thanks to better cache locality.\n155| * Default value is 14, for 16KB, which nicely fits into most L1 caches.\n156| */\n157|#ifndef LZ4_MEMORY_USAGE\n158|# define LZ4_MEMORY_USAGE LZ4_MEMORY_USAGE_DEFAULT\n159|#endif\n160|\n161|/* These are absolute limits, they should not be changed by users */\n162|#define LZ4_MEMORY_USAGE_MIN 10\n163|#define LZ4_MEMORY_USAGE_DEFAULT 14\n164|#define LZ4_MEMORY_USAGE_MAX 20\n165|\n166|#if (LZ4_MEMORY_USAGE < LZ4_MEMORY_USAGE_MIN)\n167|#  error \"LZ4_MEMORY_USAGE is too small !\"\n168|#endif\n169|\n170|#if (LZ4_MEMORY_USAGE > LZ4_MEMORY_USAGE_MAX)\n171|#  error \"LZ4_MEMORY_USAGE is too large !\"\n172|#endif\n173|\n174|/*-************************************\n175|*  Simple Functions\n176|**************************************/\n177|/*! LZ4_compress_default() :\n178| *  Compresses 'srcSize' bytes from buffer 'src'\n179| *  into already allocated 'dst' buffer of size 'dstCapacity'.\n180| *  Compression is guaranteed to succeed if 'dstCapacity' >= LZ4_compressBound(srcSize).\n181| *  It also runs faster, so it's a recommended setting.\n182| *  If the function cannot compress 'src' into a more limited 'dst' budget,\n183| *  compression stops *immediately*, and the function result is zero.\n184| *  In which case, 'dst' content is undefined (invalid).\n185| *      srcSize : max supported value is LZ4_MAX_INPUT_SIZE.\n186| *      dstCapacity : size of buffer 'dst' (which must be already allocated)\n187| *     @return  : the number of bytes written into buffer 'dst' (necessarily <= dstCapacity)\n188| *                or 0 if compression fails\n189| * Note : This function is protected against buffer overflow scenarios (never writes outside 'dst' buffer, nor read outside 'source' buffer).\n190| */\n191|LZ4LIB_API int LZ4_compress_default(const char* src, char* dst, int srcSize, int dstCapacity);\n192|\n193|/*! LZ4_decompress_safe() :\n194| * @compressedSize : is the exact complete size of the compressed block.\n195| * @dstCapacity : is the size of destination buffer (which must be already allocated),\n196| *                presumed an upper bound of decompressed size.\n197| * @return : the number of bytes decompressed into destination buffer (necessarily <= dstCapacity)\n198| *           If destination buffer is not large enough, decoding will stop and output an error code (negative value).\n199| *           If the source stream is detected malformed, the function will stop decoding and return a negative result.\n200| * Note 1 : This function is protected against malicious data packets :\n201| *          it will never writes outside 'dst' buffer, nor read outside 'source' buffer,\n202| *          even if the compressed block is maliciously modified to order the decoder to do these actions.\n203| *          In such case, the decoder stops immediately, and considers the compressed block malformed.\n204| * Note 2 : compressedSize and dstCapacity must be provided to the function, the compressed block does not contain them.\n205| *          The implementation is free to send / store / derive this information in whichever way is most beneficial.\n206| *          If there is a need for a different format which bundles together both compressed data and its metadata, consider looking at lz4frame.h instead.\n207| */\n208|LZ4LIB_API int LZ4_decompress_safe (const char* src, char* dst, int compressedSize, int dstCapacity);\n209|\n210|\n211|/*-************************************\n212|*  Advanced Functions\n213|**************************************/\n214|#define LZ4_MAX_INPUT_SIZE        0x7E000000   /* 2 113 929 216 bytes */\n215|#define LZ4_COMPRESSBOUND(isize)  ((unsigned)(isize) > (unsigned)LZ4_MAX_INPUT_SIZE ? 0 : (isize) + ((isize)/255) + 16)\n216|\n217|/*! LZ4_compressBound() :\n218|    Provides the maximum size that LZ4 compression may output in a \"worst case\" scenario (input data not compressible)\n219|    This function is primarily useful for memory allocation purposes (destination buffer size).\n220|    Macro LZ4_COMPRESSBOUND() is also provided for compilation-time evaluation (stack memory allocation for example).\n221|    Note that LZ4_compress_default() compresses faster when dstCapacity is >= LZ4_compressBound(srcSize)\n222|        inputSize  : max supported value is LZ4_MAX_INPUT_SIZE\n223|        return : maximum output size in a \"worst case\" scenario\n224|              or 0, if input size is incorrect (too large or negative)\n225|*/\n226|LZ4LIB_API int LZ4_compressBound(int inputSize);\n227|\n228|/*! LZ4_compress_fast() :\n229|    Same as LZ4_compress_default(), but allows selection of \"acceleration\" factor.\n230|    The larger the acceleration value, the faster the algorithm, but also the lesser the compression.\n231|    It's a trade-off. It can be fine tuned, with each successive value providing roughly +~3% to speed.\n232|    An acceleration value of \"1\" is the same as regular LZ4_compress_default()\n233|    Values <= 0 will be replaced by LZ4_ACCELERATION_DEFAULT (currently == 1, see lz4.c).\n234|    Values > LZ4_ACCELERATION_MAX will be replaced by LZ4_ACCELERATION_MAX (currently == 65537, see lz4.c).\n235|*/\n236|LZ4LIB_API int LZ4_compress_fast (const char* src, char* dst, int srcSize, int dstCapacity, int acceleration);\n237|\n238|\n239|/*! LZ4_compress_destSize() :\n249| *  Reverse the logic : compresses as much data as possible from 'src' buffer\n250| *  into already allocated buffer 'dst', of size >= 'dstCapacity'.\n251| *  This function either compresses the entire 'src' content into 'dst' if it's large enough,\n252| *  or fill 'dst' buffer completely with as much data as possible from 'src'.\n253| *  note: acceleration parameter is fixed to \"default\".\n254| *\n255| * *srcSizePtr : in+out parameter. LZ4_decompress_safe_partial() :\n277| *  Decompress an LZ4 compressed block, of size 'srcSize' at position 'src',\n278| *  into destination buffer 'dst' of size 'dstCapacity'.\n279| *  Up to 'targetOutputSize' bytes will be decoded.\n280| *  The function stops decoding on reaching this objective.\n281| *  This can be useful to boost performance\n282| *  whenever only the beginning of a block is required.\n283| *\n284| * @return : the number of bytes decoded in `dst` (necessarily <= targetOutputSize)\n285| *           If source stream is detected malformed, function returns a negative result.\n286| *\n287| *  Note 1 : @return can be < targetOutputSize, if compressed block contains less data.\n288| *\n289| *  Note 2 : targetOutputSize must be <= dstCapacity\n290| *\n291| *  Note 3 : this function effectively stops decoding on reaching targetOutputSize,\n292| *           so dstCapacity is kind of redundant.\n293| *           This is because in older versions of this function,\n294| *           decoding operation would still write complete sequences.\n295| *           Therefore, there was no guarantee that it would stop writing at exactly targetOutputSize,\n296| *           it could write more bytes, though only up to dstCapacity.\n297| *           Some \"margin\" used to be required for this operation to work properly.\n298| *           Thankfully, this is no longer necessary.\n299| *           The function nonetheless keeps the same signature, in an effort to preserve API compatibility.\n300| *\n301| *  Note 4 : If srcSize is the exact size of the block,\n302| *           then targetOutputSize can be any value,\n303| *           including larger than the block's decompressed size.\n304| *           The function will, at most, generate block's decompressed size.\n305| *\n306| *  Note 5 : If srcSize is _larger_ than block's compressed size,\n307| *           then targetOutputSize **MUST** be <= block's decompressed size.\n308| *           Otherwise, *silent corruption will occur*.\n309| */\n310|LZ4LIB_API int LZ4_decompress_safe_partial (const char* src, char* dst, int srcSize, int targetOutputSize, int dstCapacity);\n311|\n312|\n313|/*-*********************************************\n314|*  Streaming Compression Functions\n315|***********************************************/\n316|typedef union LZ4_stream_u LZ4_stream_t;  /* incomplete type (defined later) */\n317|\n318|/*!\n319| Note about RC_INVOKED\n320|\n321| - RC_INVOKED is predefined symbol of rc.exe (the resource compiler which is streamPtr);\n335|#endif /* !defined(LZ4_STATIC_LINKING_ONLY_DISABLE_MEMORY_ALLOCATION) */\n336|#endif\n337|\n338|/*! LZ4_resetStream_fast() : v1.9.0+\n339| *  Use this to prepare an LZ4_stream_t for a new chain of dependent blocks\n340| *  (e.g., LZ4_compress_fast_continue()).\n341| *\n342| *  An LZ4_stream_t must be initialized once before usage.\n343| *  This is automatically done when created by LZ4_createStream().\n344| *  However, should the LZ4_stream_t be simply declared on stack (for example),\n345| *  it's necessary to initialize it first, using LZ4_initStream().\n346| *\n347| *  After init, start any new stream with LZ4_resetStream_fast().\n348| *  A same LZ4_stream_t can be re-used multiple times consecutively\n349| *  and compress multiple streams,\n350| *  provided that it starts each new stream with LZ4_resetStream_fast().\n351| *\n352| *  LZ4_resetStream_fast() is much faster than LZ4_initStream(),\n353| *  but is not compatible with memory regions containing garbage data.\n354| *\n355| *  Note: it's only useful to call LZ4_resetStream_fast()\n356| *        in the context of streaming compression.\n357| *        The *extState* functions perform their own resets.\n358| *        Invoking LZ4_resetStream_fast() before is redundant, and even counterproductive.\n359| */\n360|LZ4LIB_API void LZ4_resetStream_fast (LZ4_stream_t* streamPtr);\n361|\n362|/*! LZ4_loadDict() :\n363| *  Use this function to reference a static dictionary into LZ4_stream_t.\n364| *  The dictionary must remain available during compression.\n365| *  LZ4_loadDict() triggers a reset, so any previous data will be forgotten.\n366| *  The same dictionary will have to be loaded on decompression side for successful decoding.\n367| *  Dictionary are useful for better compression of small data (KB range).\n368| *  While LZ4 itself accepts any input as dictionary, dictionary efficiency is also a topic.\n369| *  When in doubt, employ the Zstandard's Dictionary Builder.\n370| *  Loading a size of 0 is allowed, and is the same as reset.\n371| * @return : loaded dictionary size, in bytes (note: only the last 64 KB are loaded)\n372| */\n373|LZ4LIB_API int LZ4_loadDict (LZ4_stream_t* streamPtr, const char* dictionary, int dictSize);\n374|\n375|/*! LZ4_loadDictSlow() : v1.10.0+\n376| *  Same as LZ4_loadDict(),\n377| *  but uses a bit more cpu to reference the dictionary content more thoroughly.\n378| *  This is expected to slightly improve compression ratio.\n379| *  The extra-cpu cost is likely worth it if the dictionary is re-used across multiple sessions.\n380| * @return : loaded dictionary size, in bytes (note: only the last 64 KB are loaded)\n381| */\n382|LZ4LIB_API int LZ4_loadDictSlow(LZ4_stream_t* streamPtr, const char* dictionary, int dictSize);\n383|\n384|/*! LZ4_attach_dictionary() : stable since v1.10.0\n385| *\n386| *  This allows efficient re-use of a static dictionary multiple times.\n387| *\n388| *  Rather than re-loading the dictionary buffer into a working context before\n389| *  each compression, or copying a pre-loaded dictionary's LZ4_stream_t into a\n390| *  working LZ4_stream_t, this function introduces a no-copy setup mechanism,\n391| *  in which the working stream references @dictionaryStream in-place.\n392| *\n393| *  Several assumptions are made about the state of @dictionaryStream.\n394| *  Currently, only states which have been prepared by LZ4_loadDict() or\n395| *  LZ4_loadDictSlow() should be expected to work.\n396| *\n397| *  Alternatively, the provided @dictionaryStream may be NULL,\n398| *  in which case any existing dictionary stream is unset.\n399| *\n400| *  If a dictionary is provided, it replaces any pre-existing stream history.\n401| *  The dictionary contents are the only history that can be referenced and\n402| *  logically immediately precede the data compressed in the first subsequent\n403| *  compression call.\n404| *\n405| *  The dictionary will only remain attached to the working stream through the\n406| *  first compression call, at the end of which it is cleared.\n407| * @dictionaryStream stream (and source buffer) must remain in-place / accessible / unchanged\n408| *  through the completion of the compression session.\n409| *\n410| *  Note: there is no equivalent LZ4_attach_*() method on the decompression side\n411| *  because there is no initialization cost, hence no need to share the cost across multiple sessions.\n412| *  To decompress LZ4 blocks using dictionary, attached or not,\n413| *  just employ the regular LZ4_setStreamDecode() for streaming,\n414| *  or the stateless LZ4_decompress_safe_usingDict() for one-shot decompression.\n415| */\n416|LZ4LIB_API void\n417|LZ4_attach_dictionary(LZ4_stream_t* workingStream,\n418|                const LZ4_stream_t* dictionaryStream);\n419|\n420|/*! LZ4_compress_fast_continue() :\n421| *  Compress 'src' content using data from previously compressed blocks, for better compression ratio.\n422| * 'dst' buffer must be already allocated.\n423| *  If dstCapacity >= LZ4_compressBound(srcSize), compression is guaranteed to succeed, and runs faster.\n424| *\n425| * @return : size of compressed block\n426| *           or 0 if there is an error (typically, cannot fit into 'dst').\n427| *\n428| *  Note 1 : Each invocation to LZ4_compress_fast_continue() generates a new block.\n429| *           Each block has precise boundaries.\n430| *           Each block must be decompressed separately, calling LZ4_decompress_*() with relevant metadata.\n431| *           It's not possible to append blocks together and expect a single invocation of LZ4_decompress_*() to decompress them together.\n432| *\n433| *  Note 2 : The previous 64KB of source data is __assumed__ to remain present, unmodified, at same address in memory !\n434| *\n435| *  Note 3 : When input is structured as a double-buffer, each buffer can have any size, including < 64 KB.\n436| *           Make sure that buffers are separated, by at least one byte.\n437| *           This construction ensures that each block only depends on previous block.\n438| *\n439| *  Note 4 : If input buffer is a ring-buffer, it can have any size, including < 64 KB.\n440| *\n441| *  Note 5 : After an error, the stream status is undefined (invalid), it can only be reset or freed.\n442| */\n443|LZ4LIB_API int LZ4_compress_fast_continue (LZ4_stream_t* streamPtr, const char* src, char* dst, int srcSize, int dstCapacity, int acceleration);\n444|\n445|/*! LZ4_decoderRingBufferSize() : v1.8.2+\n482| *  Note : in a ring buffer scenario (optional),\n483| *  blocks are presumed decompressed next to each other\n484| *  up to the moment there is not enough remaining space for next block (remainingSize < maxBlockSize),\n485| *  at which stage it resumes from beginning of ring buffer.\n486| *  When setting such a ring buffer for streaming decompression,\n487| *  provides the minimum size of this ring buffer\n488| *  to be compatible with any source respecting maxBlockSize condition.\n489| * @return : minimum ring buffer size,\n490| *           or 0 if there is an error (invalid maxBlockSize).\n491| */\n492|LZ4LIB_API int LZ4_decoderRingBufferSize(int maxBlockSize);\n493|#define LZ4_DECODER_RING_BUFFER_SIZE(maxBlockSize) (65536 + 14 + (maxBlockSize))  /* for static allocation; maxBlockSize presumed valid */\n494|\n495|/*! LZ4_decompress_safe_continue() :\n496| *  This decoding function allows decompression of consecutive blocks in \"streaming\" mode.\n497| *  The difference with the usual independent blocks is that\n498| *  new blocks are allowed to find references into former blocks.\n499| *  A block is an unsplittable entity, and must be presented entirely to the decompression function.\n500| *  LZ4_decompress_safe_continue() only accepts one block at a time.\n501| *  It's modeled after `LZ4_decompress_safe()` and behaves similarly.\n502| *\n503| * @LZ4_streamDecode : decompression state, tracking the position in memory of past data\n504| * @compressedSize : exact complete size of one compressed block.\n505| * @dstCapacity : size of destination buffer (which must be already allocated),\n506| *                must be an upper bound of decompressed size.\n507| * @return : number of bytes decompressed into destination buffer (necessarily <= dstCapacity)\n508| *           If destination buffer is not large enough, decoding will stop and output an error code (negative value).\n509| *           If the source stream is detected malformed, the function will stop decoding and return a negative result.\n510| *\n511| *  The last 64KB of previously decoded data *must* remain available and unmodified\n512| *  at the memory position where they were previously decoded.\n513| *  If less than 64KB of data has been decoded, all the data must be present.\n514| *\n515| *  Special : if decompression side sets a ring buffer, it must respect one of the following conditions :\n516| *  - Decompression buffer size is _at least_ LZ4_decoderRingBufferSize(maxBlockSize).\n517| *    maxBlockSize is the maximum size of any single block. It can have any value > 16 bytes.\n518| *    In which case, encoding and decoding buffers do not need to be synchronized.\n519| *    Actually, data can be produced by any source compliant with LZ4 format specification, and respecting maxBlockSize.\n520| *  - Synchronized mode :\n521| *    Decompression buffer size is _exactly_ the same as compression buffer size,\n522| *    and follows exactly same update rule (block boundaries at same positions),\n523| *    and decoding function is provided with exact decompressed size of each block (exception for last block of the stream),\n524| *    _then_ decoding & encoding ring buffer can have any size, including small ones ( < 64 KB).\n525| *  - Decompression buffer is larger than encoding buffer, by a minimum of maxBlockSize more bytes.\n526| *    In which case, encoding and decoding buffers do not need to be synchronized,\n527| *    and encoding ring buffer can have any size, including small ones ( < 64 KB).\n528| *\n529| *  Whenever these conditions are not possible,\n530| *  save the last 64KB of decoded data into a safe buffer where it can't be modified during decompression,\n531| *  then indicate where this data is saved using LZ4_setStreamDecode(), before decompressing next block.\n532|*/\n533|LZ4LIB_API int\n534|LZ4_decompress_safe_continue (LZ4_streamDecode_t* LZ4_streamDecode,\n535|                        const char* src, char* dst,\n536|                        int srcSize, int dstCapacity);\n537|\n538|\n539|/*! LZ4_compress_destSize_extState() : introduced in v1.10.0\n616| *  Same as LZ4_compress_destSize(), but using an externally allocated state.\n617| *  Also: exposes @acceleration\n618| */\n619|int LZ4_compress_destSize_extState(void* state, const char* src, char* dst, int* srcSizePtr, int targetDstSize, int acceleration);\n620|\n621|/*! In-place compression and decompression\n622| *\n623| * It's possible to have input and output sharing the same buffer,\n624| * for highly constrained memory environments.\n625| * In both cases, it requires input to lay at the end of the buffer,\n626| * and decompression to start at beginning of the buffer.\n627| * Buffer size must feature some margin, hence be larger than final size.\n628| *\n629| * |<------------------------buffer--------------------------------->|\n630| *                             |<-----------compressed data--------->|\n631| * |<-----------decompressed size------------------>|\n632| *                                                  |<----margin---->|\n633| *\n634| * This technique is more useful for decompression,\n635| * since decompressed size is typically larger,\n636| * and margin is short.\n637| *\n638| * In-place decompression will work inside any buffer\n639| * which size is >= LZ4_DECOMPRESS_INPLACE_BUFFER_SIZE(decompressedSize).\n640| * This presumes that decompressedSize > compressedSize.\n641| * Otherwise, it means compression actually expanded data,\n642| * and it would be more efficient to store such data with a flag indicating it's not compressed.\n643| * This can happen when data is not compressible (already compressed, or encrypted).\n644| *\n645| * For in-place compression, margin is larger, as it must be able to cope with both\n646| * history preservation, requiring input data to remain unmodified up to LZ4_DISTANCE_MAX,\n647| * and data expansion, which can happen when input is not compressible.\n648| * As a consequence, buffer size requirements are much higher,\n649| * and memory savings offered by in-place compression are more limited.\n650| *\n651| * There are ways to limit this cost for compression :\n652| * - Reduce history size, by modifying LZ4_DISTANCE_MAX.\n653| *   Note that it is a compile-time constant, so all compressions will apply this limit.\n654| *   Lower values will reduce compression ratio, except when input_size < LZ4_DISTANCE_MAX,\n655| *   so it's a reasonable trick when inputs are known to be small.\n656| * - Require the compressor to deliver a \"maximum compressed size\".\n657| *   This is the `dstCapacity` parameter in `LZ4_compress*()`.\n658| *   When this size is < LZ4_COMPRESSBOUND(inputSize), then compression can fail,\n659| *   in which case, the return code will be 0 (zero).\n660| *   The caller must be ready for these cases to happen,\n661| *   and typically design a backup scheme to send data uncompressed.\n662| * The combination of both techniques can significantly reduce\n663| * the amount of margin required for in-place compression.\n664| *\n665| * In-place compression can work in any buffer\n666| * which size is >= (maxCompressedSize)\n667| * with maxCompressedSize == LZ4_COMPRESSBOUND(srcSize) for guaranteed compression success.\n668| * LZ4_COMPRESS_INPLACE_BUFFER_SIZE() depends on both maxCompressedSize and LZ4_DISTANCE_MAX,\n669| * so it's possible to reduce memory requirements by playing with them.\n670| */\n671|\n672|#define LZ4_DECOMPRESS_INPLACE_MARGIN(compressedSize)          (((compressedSize) >> 8) + 32)\n673|#define LZ4_DECOMPRESS_INPLACE_BUFFER_SIZE(decompressedSize)   ((decompressedSize) + LZ4_DECOMPRESS_INPLACE_MARGIN(decompressedSize))  /**< note: presumes that compressedSize < decompressedSize. note2: margin is overestimated a bit, since it could use compressedSize instead */\n674|\n675|#ifndef LZ4_DISTANCE_MAX   /* history window size; can be user-defined at compile time */\n676|#  define LZ4_DISTANCE_MAX 65535   /* set to maximum value by default */\n677|#endif\n678|\n679|#define LZ4_COMPRESS_INPLACE_MARGIN                           (LZ4_DISTANCE_MAX + 32)   /* LZ4_DISTANCE_MAX can be safely replaced by srcSize when it's smaller */\n680|#define LZ4_COMPRESS_INPLACE_BUFFER_SIZE(maxCompressedSize)   ((maxCompressedSize) + LZ4_COMPRESS_INPLACE_MARGIN)  /**< maxCompressedSize is generally LZ4_COMPRESSBOUND(inputSize), but can be set to any lower value, with the risk that compression can fail (return code 0(zero)) */\n681|\n682|#endif   /* LZ4_STATIC_3504398509 */\n683|#endif   /* LZ4_STATIC_LINKING_ONLY */\n684|\n685|\n686|\n687|#ifndef LZ4_H_98237428734687\n688|#define Obsolete LZ4_decompress_fast variants (since v1.9.0) :\n839| *  These functions used to be faster than LZ4_decompress_safe(),\n840| *  but this is no longer the case. They are now slower.\n841| *  This is because LZ4_decompress_fast() doesn't know the input size,\n842| *  and therefore must progress more cautiously into the input buffer to not read beyond the end of block.\n843| *  On top of that `LZ4_decompress_fast()` is not protected vs malformed or malicious inputs, making it a security liability.\n844| *  As a consequence, LZ4_decompress_fast() is strongly discouraged, and deprecated.\n845| *\n846| *  The last remaining LZ4_decompress_fast() specificity is that\n847| *  it can decompress a block without knowing its compressed size.\n848| *  Such functionality can be achieved in a more secure manner\n849| *  by employing LZ4_decompress_safe_partial().\n850| *\n851| *  Parameters:\n852| *  originalSize : is the uncompressed size to regenerate.\n853| *                 `dst` must be already allocated, its size must be >= 'originalSize' bytes.\n854| * @return : number of bytes read from source buffer (== compressed size).\n855| *           The function expects to finish at block's end exactly.\n856| *           If the source stream is detected malformed, the function stops decoding and returns a negative result.\n857| *  note : LZ4_decompress_fast*() requires originalSize. Thanks to this information, it never writes past the output buffer.\n858| *         However, since it doesn't know its 'src' size, it may read an unknown amount of input, past input buffer bounds.\n859| *         Also, since match offsets are not validated, match reads from 'src' may underflow too.\n860| *         These issues never happen if input (compressed) data is correct.\n861| *         But they may happen if input data is invalid (error or intentional tampering).\n862| *         As a consequence, use these functions in trusted environments with trusted data **only**.\n863| */\n864|LZ4_DEPRECATED(\"This function is deprecated and unsafe.
